  PER-PERSON DETECTION
  The floor is where a person can JUST be told apart
  from a guesser -- at exactly the floor you catch them
  about half the time. The weight below is where you
  catch them 80% of the time, which
  is the number to plan with.

  CROSSOVER: IS FOG BIGGER THAN COLOUR, IN ONE
  PERSON?
  The floors above each ask whether ONE weight
  differs from zero. This asks whether the TWO
  weights differ from each other, which is the
  actual crossover hypothesis -- and it is a harder
  question, because the noise on a difference is
  bigger than the noise on either part.

    simulated at a baseline weight of 0.5 on both cues (an assumption)
    scatter of the difference      0.331
    (the two scatters in quadrature 0.318 -- close, so the
     usual back-of-envelope is a fair guide here)
    critical values                -0.71 .. +0.66
    gap caught 80% of the time        0.95
      Compare that with the single-feature
      numbers above. A gap this size is the
      price of the CROSSOVER claim; a person can
      clear the fog floor, miss the colour
      floor, and still not clear this.

   true_gap  mean_est_gap  sd_est_gap  detected

  ADDITIVITY: DO THE TWO CUES SIMPLY ADD?
  What the `together` and `conflict` trials are
  really for. gamma is the proportional change in
  the combined evidence at the largest differences:
  +0.5 means the two cues together carry 50% more
  weight than their sum, -0.5 means 50% less, and 0
  is additive. The two-weight model used everywhere
  else ASSUMES gamma = 0, so it cannot test this.

    additivity-term VIF            1.43
    critical values                -0.30 .. +1.21
    gamma caught 80% of the time      4.03
      i.e. the cues would have to combine this
      much more strongly than additively before
      ONE participant's data could show it.

   true_gamma  mean_b_int  sd_b_int  detected
         0.00       0.105     0.378     0.042

  ADDITIVITY ACROSS PEOPLE
  The mixed trials pool: n participants give n times
  as many of them. This is the same question asked
  of n people at once, and it is the only form in
  which it is answerable.

   n_people  informing_trials  crit_lo  crit_hi  false_alarm  gamma_at_80%
          1               180   -0.278    1.310        0.077         4.000
          5               900   -0.143    0.211        0.047         0.641
         10              1800   -0.107    0.124        0.060         0.401
         15              2700   -0.085    0.109        0.043         0.367

  Treats everyone as sharing one gamma, so it is an
  UPPER BOUND on what a mixed-effects fit would
  manage -- real people differ, and that spread is
  not simulated. Read it as 'no better than this'.

4. THE DECISION RULE FOR THE EXISTENCE CLAIM
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  The claim is that SOME people weight the feature.
  With a per-person threshold calibrated on guessers,
  a handful of detections is expected under any null,
  so the rule has to say how many are enough.

  THREE FAMILIES WERE COMPARED:
    count-absolute      at least k detections and at
                        most m in the wrong direction.
                        DEGENERATES with sample size --
                        a fixed m that is safe at n = 8
                        is almost never met at n = 120,
                        so power rises and then falls.
    count-proportional  the same, with m scaled to n.
    sign test           the detections' own asymmetry.
                        Its false-claim rate is BOUNDED
                        BY alpha for any symmetric
                        null, whatever the per-tail
                        rate, which is a stronger
                        guarantee than the others have.
                        It cannot fire below 5
                        detections at all.

  TWO ADMISSIBILITY STANDARDS, both reported:
    practical  the rule must hold against a guesser
               and against taste spreads of 0.25x and
               0.5x each feature's own threshold.
    strict     and against 1.0x as well, i.e. a
               population whose idle preferences are
               as large as the detection threshold.

  sign test, worst false-claim rate        0.0371 vs alpha 0.05 (bound holds)
  sign test needs at least                 5 detections to fire at all

  the most powerful admissible rule, per family:

5. WHY THE STRICT STANDARD, AND NOT THE CHEAPER OPTION
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  THE REGISTERED STANDARD IS STRICT, chosen outright and
  not from the data. This section is the evidence for
  that choice.

  The cheaper option was costed first. The practical
  standard has more power, and it is admissible
  against a guesser and against taste spreads of
  0.25x and 0.5x -- but it reaches 0.363 against a 1x
  spread, which it is not required to hold against.
  The proposal was to let the CONTROL GROUP decide:
  estimate the taste spread, and if it comes back
  above 0.75x of a feature's own threshold, evaluate
  that feature's claim with the strict rule instead.

  IT DOES NOT WORK, AND THE REASON IS GENERAL. The
  rule would be chosen from data, so neither branch's
  rate is the procedure's rate:

      combined = P(strict) x FP_strict
               + P(practical) x FP_practical

  The branch is decided by the CONTROL sample and the
  rule applied to the PARTICIPANT sample -- different
  people -- so the two factorise exactly and the
  combined rate is computable rather than simulated
  end to end.

  The combined rate PEAKS AT THE CHECK'S OWN
  BOUNDARY. When the true spread sits on 0.75x, the
  estimate lands either side about half the time, so
  half the studies apply the practical rule to a
  population it was never required to hold against.
  Past the boundary the check almost always fires and
  the rate falls back; below it the practical rule is
  admissible anyway. The worst case is the middle.

  AND IT DOES NOT IMPROVE WITH MORE CONTROLS. Below
  the minimum the procedure forces the strict rule and
  is safe by construction, so recruiting the thirtieth
  control moves the study OUT of a safe regime and
  into the noisy-branch one.

  control check, null rates                no-strategy rates taken from the RULE SWEEP at spreads 0.25x, 0.5x, 0.75x, 1x; measured here at 1.25x -- the sweep does not simulate it
  control check, strategy pushes to strict 10/10 cells monotone
  variant B power cost against A           +0.000 to +0.349 at the summary cell
  procedure variant A (as decided), worst rate 0.1154
  procedure variant B (admissible at the boundary), worst rate 0.0483
  procedure variant C (conservative branch), worst rate 0.0546

  THE CONTROL-BASED SPREAD ESTIMATE IS STILL
  REPORTED, DESCRIPTIVELY. It is the only measurement
  of the quantity every rule is judged against, and
  the next section says how well it can be measured at
  all. No registered claim depends on it.

  The strategy test is reported with it. Taste is
  symmetric by construction and the shared-concept
  strategy is not, so an excess of predicted over
  opposite classifications among the controls says the
  spread estimate is inflated. Since nothing branches
  on it any more, it is a diagnostic: it tells a
  reader whether the control group behaved like the
  null the rules were judged against.

6. THE SAME CLAIMS, STATED BEHAVIOURALLY
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  THE PRIMARY FORM OF EVERY EXISTENCE CLAIM.

  A per-SD weight is not comparable between the two
  features on this list. The informative colour trials
  are about 1.0 corpus SD apart and the fog trials
  about 0.62, so the same weight produces a much
  smaller choice bias on fog, and the fog threshold
  sits correspondingly higher -- the ratio of the two
  thresholds is the square root of the ratio of the
  two sums of squares, which is arithmetic, not an
  asymmetry of the design.

  Stated as a choice probability on a typical
  informative trial, the two features' claims are
  equivalent: the residual between them is a few
  thousandths across the grid. The per-SD table is
  kept beside it because the weights are what the
  model fits, but THE BEHAVIOURAL COLUMN IS THE CLAIM.

  H1 vs H2 at equal behaviour              median gap 0.010, worst 0.230 (46/192 cells > 0.05)

7. PREVALENCE
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  The rules above answer 'did anyone?'. This answers
  'what share?', on the same detections.

  theta = gamma(1 - beta) + (1 - gamma)alpha: the
  observed detection rate is the true prevalence times
  the sensitivity plus the rest times the
  false-positive rate (Ince et al., eLife 2021).

  TWO VARIANTS, and the registered one is the second:
    fixed alpha      alpha is the calibrated per-tail
                     rate. Correct only if the
                     population really does guess.
    estimated alpha  alpha is estimated from the
                     OPPOSITE-direction
                     classifications in the same data.
                     Under a taste null those are
                     inflated too, so the estimate
                     moves with the contamination
                     instead of ignoring it.

  prevalence moment estimator              unbiased unclipped (worst 0.0000); clipped worst 0.2901 at a low prevalence with a wide taste spread
  prevalence FP under a null, colour       0.093 worst (spread 1x, n=10), nominal 0.05
  prevalence FP under a null, fog          0.261 worst (spread 1x, n=15), nominal 0.05
  prevalence, registered form              DESCRIPTIVE for both features: estimator + interval, no 'excludes zero' inference (underpowered as a test, and its size is not uniform across taste nulls)
  prevalence, rejected alpha_hat bias      E[alpha_hat]/alpha_true = 0.467-0.900, i.e. (1 - prevalence)
  prevalence variants                      5 per feature, alpha 0.025-0.185

  THE DECISION-RELEVANT NUMBER: how many of n people
  have to be classified as detecting the feature
  before the posterior's lower bound clears zero --
  i.e. before 'some people do' is supportable at all.

  (95% HPDI; '-' means no count up to n is enough.
   Each variant assumes a different false-positive
   rate: a pure guesser, or a population whose idle
   preferences have the stated spread relative to that
   feature's own threshold.)

    n                                  8   10  12  14  15  16  20  25  30  40  60  80  120
    feature variant             alpha                                                     
    colour  assumed_2.5pc       0.0250   2   2   2   2   2   3   3   3   3   4   5   6   7
            assumed_taste_0.25x 0.0360   2   2   3   3   3   3   3   4   4   5   6   7   9
            assumed_taste_0.5x  0.0779   3   3   3   4   4   4   5   5   6   7   9  12  16
            assumed_taste_0.75x 0.1331   3   4   4   5   5   5   6   7   8  10  14  17  24
            assumed_taste_1x    0.1820   4   5   5   6   6   6   7   9  10  12  17  22  30
    fog     assumed_2.5pc       0.0250   2   2   2   2   2   3   3   3   3   4   5   6   7
            assumed_taste_0.25x 0.0395   2   2   3   3   3   3   3   4   4   5   6   7  10
            assumed_taste_0.5x  0.0799   3   3   3   4   4   4   5   5   6   7  10  12  16
            assumed_taste_0.75x 0.1351   3   4   4   5   5   5   6   7   8  10  14  17  24
            assumed_taste_1x    0.1855   4   5   5   6   6   6   7   9  10  12  17  22  31

  The jump between variants is the whole point. A rule
  calibrated on guessers needs very few detections; the
  same rule needs several more once the null population
  is allowed idle preferences, and the registered
  variant below does not have to guess which it is.

  THE REGISTERED VARIANT ASSUMES NO alpha AT ALL.

  Under any symmetric null a non-responder is as
  likely to be classified in the opposite direction as
  in the predicted one, so the OPPOSITE count measures
  the contamination directly. A responder contributes
  almost nothing to it -- a real preference does not
  also look backwards, measured at 0.0000 on this
  design.

  The model is the one above, written in the rate the
  data can see:

      c      = (1 - prevalence) x alpha
      p_pred = prevalence x sensitivity + c
      p_opp  =                            c

  with (predicted, opposite, neither) trinomial in
  those, and the posterior on the prevalence
  marginalised over c.

  A moment estimator falls out of the same algebra and
  needs no alpha either, because the non-responders'
  contributions to the two counts cancel:

      prevalence_hat = (predicted - opposite)
                       / (n x sensitivity)

  exactly unbiased before clipping. 'E_moment' below is
  that estimator; 'E_mode' and the interval are the
  posterior's. 'P_excludes_zero' at a true prevalence
  of 0 IS the prevalence claim's own false-positive
  rate, computed with nothing assumed about the null.

  WHAT THIS REPLACED, AND WHY. An earlier version
  estimated alpha as (opposite count) / n and fed it to
  the fixed-alpha posterior. Only the NON-responders
  produce opposite classifications, while that
  denominator counts everyone, so the estimate
  converges to (1 - prevalence) x alpha -- biased low
  by the very quantity being estimated.
  'old_bias_factor' is E[alpha_hat] / alpha_true for
  each cell; it equals (n - responders) / n to three
  decimals, which identifies the mechanism rather than
  leaving it as an unexplained discrepancy.

  PARAMETERISING BY alpha INSTEAD DOES NOT WORK, and
  the attempt is on the record: with a prior on alpha
  and a joint posterior over (prevalence, alpha), the
  two are not separately identified at these sample
  sizes -- p_opp carries a (1 - prevalence) factor, so
  a prevalence near 1 drives p_opp to zero, fits an
  observed opposite count of zero perfectly and
  maximises p_pred at the same time. With a true
  prevalence of 0 and a 1x taste spread that
  parameterisation returned a posterior mode of 0.86.
  In (prevalence, c) the opposite count pins c down on
  its own and the ridge is gone.

  feature  effect_sd  true_prevalence  realised_prevalence  taste_spread  alpha_true  c_true  n  E_moment_raw  E_moment  E_mode  E_hpdi_lo  E_hpdi_hi  P_excludes_zero  E_old_alpha_hat  old_bias_factor

9. THE JOINT LAPSE MODEL: A NEGATIVE RESULT
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  RECORDED BECAUSE THE GATE'S DESIGN DEPENDS ON IT.

  The obvious way to handle inattention is to fit it:
  p = (1 - lambda)logistic(Xb) + lambda/2, three
  parameters per person. Tried, and it does not
  identify on this design.

  The fitter was verified first, so this is a property
  of the design and not of the code: at 7200 trials
  per person it recovers the weight and beats the
  likelihood at the true parameters. At 180 trials the
  log-likelihood moves by under a tenth of a unit
  across lambda from 0 to 0.4, and a group-level
  lambda shared across 30 people leaves everything
  from 0 to 0.25 within two units of the best.

  The reason is structural: lapses attenuate the
  fitted weight by about (1 - lambda), and that
  attenuation is their ONLY signature in choice data --
  exactly degenerate with a smaller true weight. A
  binary 2AFC task with no confidence ratings has no
  second moment to break the tie.

  So C1 does not get a lapse-corrected sensitivity
  analysis. It gets the gate instead, which uses the
  catch trials -- a measurement the weights cannot
  imitate, because a catch trial has no images and no
  preference can produce a failure on one.

  weight for 80% detection, colour / fog (lapse 0) 0.45 / 0.71
  the same at lapse 0.2                    0.55 / 0.94
  lapse model recovery                     NOT USABLE: lambda bias <= +0.1860, r(w, lambda) <= +0.489, SD inflation 16.53x
  gate sensitivity, controls lapse more    0.995-1.000; 0.950-1.000 on catch alone
  C1 false positive, controls lapse more   0.287 ungated, 0.000 gated
  C1 false positive, matched lapses        0.060 (nominal 0.05)
  C1 secondary false positive, controls lapse more 0.137 worst, 0.001 after the RT re-check, vs 0.287 ungated (nominal 0.05)
  C1 secondary power, controls lapse more  0.119-1.000 over the realistic sizes

10. THE ATTENTION GATE
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  WHAT IT IS FOR. A control group that lapses more
  than the participants has its weights attenuated
  toward zero, which widens exactly the gap C1 looks
  for. Nothing in the choice data distinguishes that
  from a real effect, and the lapse model above
  cannot either.

  TWO MEASURES, Holm-corrected across the two so the
  gate's own false-alarm rate is the alpha it claims:
    catch failures   the primary measure. Needs
                     nothing but the catch block.
    fast responses   the per-person share of image
                     trials answered under 300 ms,
                     fixed in advance. A trial needs a
                     saccade and the fixation to
                     programme it (about 200 ms) plus
                     evidence accumulation before a
                     response can be initiated, so a
                     faster answer cannot have
                     involved comparing the two
                     pictures. It is an implausibility
                     floor, NOT an engagement
                     threshold.

  THE SIMULATED SENSITIVITY IS AN UPPER BOUND. It
  assumes people lapse on catch trials at the same
  rate as on image trials (p_fail = 0.75 x lambda, from
  four live keys). A catch screen has no pictures and
  one line of text, so someone skimming may well
  attend to it more; if they lapse less there, the
  gate is less sensitive than these numbers.

  THE RESPONSE-TIME CUT-OFF MUST NOT BE RE-TUNED. It
  is reasoned from saccade latency, not measured on
  this task. Report the first participants' response
  time distribution against it; if the mode sits near
  the cut-off, the cut is wrong for this task and the
  measure should be DROPPED, not adjusted.

  gate false alarm, worst cell             0.051 (nominal 0.05)
  gate sensitivity, controls lapse more    0.995-1.000; 0.950-1.000 on catch alone
  gate false alarm, over C1's cells        0.038 (nominal 0.05)

                                                        fires  fires_catch  fires_rt  catch_alone

11. C1: THE GROUP COMPARISON
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Two one-sided Mann-Whitney tests on the per-person
  weights, H1 (fog) and H2 (colour), Holm-corrected
  across the pair.

  RANK-BASED, so no cap is needed. A separable
  participant's Firth weight is finite but means 'at
  least very large'; a mean-based test would compare a
  number the data does not pin down, while a rank does
  not care whether the top weight is 8 or 40. The
  strongest responders stay in.

  THE CONTROLS ARE NOT NON-RESPONDERS. A control can
  work out that the experiment is about edited images
  and pick the more edited one, which loads
  positively on BOTH features and eats the contrast C1
  rests on. The concept is not secret, so the strategy
  is simulated at the SAME RATE IN BOTH GROUPS; what
  appears only in the participants is the remembered
  experience.

  THE REGISTERED VERDICT. The gate is tested first.
  When it fires, C1 IS REPORTED AS INCONCLUSIVE --
  neither a positive nor a negative -- and the
  pre-registered secondary below is reported beside
  it with its realised sample sizes.

  C1 false positive, controls lapse more   0.287 ungated, 0.000 gated
  C1 false positive, matched lapses        0.060 (nominal 0.05)
  C1 secondary false positive at the realistic sizes 0.072 worst vs 0.158 ungated (nominal 0.05)
  C1 secondary false positive, controls lapse more 0.137 worst, 0.001 after the RT re-check, vs 0.287 ungated (nominal 0.05)
  C1 secondary power, controls lapse more  0.119-1.000 over the realistic sizes
  C1 zero-catch secondary, surviving controls 2.5-120.0, usable in 5%-100% of studies
  C1 cells                                 4641
  C1 power depends on share and strategy   0.94-0.98 at full share with no strategy, 0.28-0.81 at half share with a quarter using the strategy

  (c) THE SECONDARY: C1 on the people with NO catch
      failures, in both groups.

      Pre-registered rather than post-hoc matching:
      the rule is fixed before the data arrive, so the
      subsample cannot be chosen for preserving the
      result. It works only because lapse rates differ
      BETWEEN people -- a clean catch record is
      evidence of a low personal rate. If everyone in
      a group lapsed at the group mean it would select
      a random subset with the same mean and fix
      nothing.

      IT REDUCES THE ARTEFACT WITHOUT REMOVING IT.
      Selecting on a clean record leaves a residual
      lapse gap, and the power to detect that residual
      grows with the sample size and with the size of
      the weights. So its false-positive rate is at
      nominal at the sizes above and drifts up in the
      larger cells and at high strategy rates -- the
      table in (a) and the breakdown in the console
      output give both.

      THE RESPONSE-TIME RE-CHECK IS A DIAGNOSTIC, NOT
      A SECOND GATE. The subsample's own fast-response
      test fires in almost every study where the
      controls really lapse more -- with or without a
      real effect, since response times do not depend
      on the weights -- so gating on it would set the
      secondary's power to zero in every cell. That
      would amount to registering that C1 is
      unanswerable whenever the groups' attention
      differs at all. A fired re-check instead says
      the residual is present and the secondary's own
      result carries the inflation above.

      what survives, and what it is worth:

      this is the binding constraint and the secondary
      is often simply unavailable.

12. WHERE THE FROZEN DRAW SITS AMONG STEP 6'S DRAWS
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Every registered figure is a property of ONE draw of ONE
  version. This section says whether that draw was a
  typical one, which a reader cannot otherwise tell.

  Step 6's per-draw tables are outside the sealed package,
  so their numbers are quoted here -- this report is what
  survives.

  Three things have to line up, and the frozen draw is NOT
  found by its seed:
    the version    from FROZEN.json, not assumed. Step 6's
                   tables hold one row per draw PER
                   VERSION, so ranking without that filter
                   compares the frozen draw with other
                   versions' lists rather than with the
                   other draws of its own.
    the draw       by its SHIPPING flag. Step 5 builds a
                   shipping draw plus numbered replicates,
                   and step 6 records the replicates' seeds
                   from their folder names but not the
                   shipping draw's, which is step 5's
                   RANDOM_SEED. The frozen list is the
                   shipping draw, so its seed cell is
                   empty and FROZEN_SEED (42) is a
                   cross-check, not the key.
    the condition  the detection table holds several rows
                   per draw, one per simulated sensitivity
                   and participant type, so each quantity
                   is ranked within its own condition.

  Nothing is reported unless all three resolve. A missing
  percentile is a gap; a plausible one computed from the
  wrong rows is worse.

  frozen version: 'both' (from FROZEN.json)
  the frozen list is step 6's SHIPPING draw (step 7a froze the unnumbered
  folder), so it is located by its shipping flag, not by seed 42

    17 of 25 ranked quantities put the frozen draw in the middle
    half of 'both''s draws; 1 in the outer 10%.

    A draw at an extreme is not disqualifying -- it was chosen before any

13. REPRODUCING THE PRE-FREEZE RUN
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  ONE-DIRECTIONAL ON PURPOSE. The old figures came
  from the pre-freeze list AND from DETECTION_SIDED =
  "one", so reproducing them means running that
  configuration, not the registered one. A reader
  comparing the two threshold tables would otherwise
  conclude the thresholds had moved.

  A difference here is not automatically a bug: the
  list changed at the freeze, and the predictors
  changed units before it. The check exists so that
  any difference is NAMED rather than discovered
  later.

          quantity  this_run
   analysed trials  180.0000
     r(predictors)    0.1107
  threshold colour    0.5193
     threshold fog    0.4903

14. THE FIGURES
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Four figures, each drawn from a table computed
  above and saved beside it as a CSV, so any point can
  be checked without re-running anything.

  Every series carries a colour AND a marker AND a
  line style. The palette is Okabe-Ito, validated
  rather than chosen by eye -- worst adjacent-pair
  separation 11.0 under deuteranopia -- but one of the
  four has a contrast ratio of 2.98:1 against white,
  under a 3:1 target, and a registration document gets
  printed and photocopied. Colour alone would not
  survive that.

  (a) existence_power.png
      The existence claim against sample size, by
      responder share, one panel per feature. Drawn at
      the most powerful rule the REGISTERED standard
      admits at each size, so it is what the
      registered analysis would actually achieve --
      not the best rule in the sweep.

      Two panels rather than one, because H1 and H2
      have different thresholds and a shared panel
      invites reading a gap between them that is
      arithmetic rather than asymmetry. Figure (c)
      makes that comparison properly.

  (b) c1_power.png
      C1 against sample size, at matched lapse
      rates (so the gate is quiet and the primary C1
      is what is drawn) against 30 controls. One row
      of panels per responder share, one line per
      strategy rate.

      Faceted rather than drawn as one legend because
      share x strategy is six series and only four
      colours are assigned -- a fifth is never
      generated. Faceting also puts the comparison the
      figure exists for, what the strategy costs,
      inside a panel rather than across a crowded key.

      The strategy rate is on the figure because it is
      the parameter that decides whether C1 is worth
      running. C1 is better powered than the existence
      claims ONLY at full responder share with no
      strategy; at half share with a quarter of both
      groups using the strategy it is underpowered at
      every realistic size. A curve without those two
      numbers on it would be misleading, which is why
      they are in the subtitle.

  (c) equal_behaviour.png
      H1 against H2 with the identity line, each point
      one scenario with both claims set to the same
      choice probability on a typical informative
      trial. Equivalence is the diagonal, so it can be
      seen rather than inferred from two curves, and
      the diverging cells are marked.

  (d) gate.png
      The attention gate, two panels, one axis each.
      Left is what the gate COSTS: how often it fires
      when the groups are matched, which makes C1
      inconclusive for nothing. Right is what it buys,
      with the catch measure drawn beside the combined
      gate -- because response times might turn out
      uninformative, and the catch measure is what the
      gate rests on if they do.

      Each panel shows the gate at its least
      flattering: the worst case over the
      response-time sweep on the left, the least often
      fired on the right.

    written: existence_power.png
    written: c1_power.png
    written: equal_behaviour.png
    written: gate.png

15. STOPPING RULE: A SINGLE FINAL ANALYSIS
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  NOT SEQUENTIALLY MONITORED. The analysis is run ONCE,
  at the end of recruitment, which ends at a maximum N or
  an end date fixed in advance -- whichever comes first,
  both set before the first participant.

  The reason is the primary tests. The existence claims
  and C1 are frequentist: a per-person threshold
  calibrated to a fixed per-tail rate, a counting rule
  whose false-claim rate is computed against that rate,
  and rank tests at a fixed alpha. Every one of those
  rates is the rate of a SINGLE test. Looking repeatedly
  and stopping when the answer arrives inflates all of
  them, and the correction -- an alpha-spending function
  with the looks declared in advance -- would have to be
  registered too, and would cost power this design cannot
  spare at ten to twenty participants.

  The Bayes factors reported here are not affected in the
  same way, and an earlier version of this pipeline
  simulated sequential stopping on them. That is retired
  with the script that did it (see the header): a study
  whose primary tests cannot be monitored sequentially
  gains nothing registrable from a secondary measure that
  can.

  Nothing in this report assumes more than one look.

16. WHAT TO DO WITH THIS
----------------------------------------------------------------------
  If the smallest detectable individual weight is larger
  than the effect you expect, the exploratory per-person
  analysis cannot work however clean the data. The fixes,
  in order of how far back they send you:
    more trials per type        -> step 5 only
    different type proportions  -> step 5 only
    larger differences          -> steps 1 and 3, re-render

  The group-level power is driven by the number of PEOPLE,
  not by the trial list, so it cannot be fixed in the
  pipeline at all.
